# Print and packaging artwork inventory

Audit and implementation: 26 October 2026, `/opt/stacks/landing-art`, branch `art-gen`. Baseline HEAD: `3f45739f2254a1112bb9985d18f746fe883abaae` (clean at start). The detailed item rows below preserve the baseline source audit; the delivery table immediately below supersedes their historical pending/wiring statuses. No deployment, commit, push, production data or other worktree changes are part of this handoff.

## Delivery status — continuation after main v2.56.0 (27 October)

There are now **116 PrintArt keys / 104 unique local URLs, all present**, across **122 retained/new bitmap files**. This continuation adds **17 accepted generated outputs: 16 original scenes and one revised cover**, saved in `print-v4` (346,706 bytes). Eighteen tool outputs were reviewed; the first football back scene was rejected for brand-like sleeve stripes and replaced. Cumulative delivery: **81 accepted generated outputs / 93 new versioned WebPs (2,122,094 bytes)**, including 12 monthly crops/reuses. Seven monthly subjects reuse prior text-free generated originals; five reuse the earlier batch. The old 29 files and superseded football painting remain retained, not overwritten. Titles, spines, dates, prices, recipient names and player writing stay native text.

The five merge conflicts are resolved in the files, preserving main's surface-size/detail table, models and parody wording. The Git index remains for the manager to stage/finish; this worker does not write Git metadata or version releases. Startup loading is parser-deferred with a 60-second fetch timeout; the full-room rerun now reports no missing generated mappings. PM SVG rasters use `HouseKit.tex`, retaining authored pixels while respecting Potato/Low texture limits and `setAniso`. Final browser results and remaining limits are recorded below.

These remain procedural, shared or absent as described. Generated backgrounds must not replace player-created content. Source existence and unit checks are not a claim of in-game visual approval; final test results are recorded below.

The following “Current registry refresh” section and item tables are the **earlier audit snapshot**, retained to show each original source and proposed target. Missing-file counts and “pending/unwired” claims there are historical; use the delivery table above and the actual PrintArt.files map for current targets. Proposed folders such as pc-v2/packaging-v1 were not created.

## Current registry refresh — authoritative targets (baseline snapshot)

Main-agent update incorporated: `PrintArt.files` now defines **88 exact keys / 76 unique URLs**. At this read, **20 mappings point to existing files and 68 point to files not yet present**. Existence is a source/filesystem observation, not a browser decode result. New generation folders are **`box-v2`, `print-v3`, `magazines-v2`, `posters-v4`**. Keep all old sets; do not overwrite them.

All **23 exact console IDs** are registered: 17 new `box-v2` targets plus six mappings reusing `box-v1`/`print-v2`. All **12 PC game keys are now registered**, including late additions **`pc.podracer → print-v3/pod.webp`** and **`pc.grotlings → print-v3/grotlings.webp`**. Additional late mappings are `pc.cover7 → magazines-v2/07.webp` and `pc.dx61 → print-v3/desktop.webp`. The registry was re-read after these additions; targets are authoritative, while files remain pending at this check.

Magazine covers now use **native `PrintArt.magazine` composition over text-free `magazines-v2/MM.webp` art** in the reader/HouseArt paths. Posters use text-free `posters-v4` art plus native titles. Legacy lettered sets stay catalogued for provenance, but are not current generation targets. Console labels/cases and PM SVG consumers now use the shared registry and embedded generated layers.

Other-house packaging is itemised below: **all nine shopping items**, Shopwise carrier bags, washing-up liquid, ketchup, Assorted biscuit tin, Tea Chest/Xmas Decs storage cartons, paint tins, pencil/floppy storage labels, Dad's sweets-tin mention, sweet wrappers, competition parcel and Pixel Post parcels. Except the seven reserved pack keys above and registered catalogue/media art, these remain source-drawn or absent and need explicit mapping if in the main generation batch.

New statuses: **registered-existing** = registry URL exists (use/wiring described separately); **registered-pending** = consumer/registry prepared but file absent; **registered-unwired / registered-pending-unwired** = registry entry exists but corresponding physical consumer still absent or not converted; **expected-registry-addition** = main-agent-announced addition not yet in files; **unregistered** = source surface has no current matching files key. These statuses supersede historical source-only assessments for updated rows.

## Brief and interpretation

Every authored print/packaging illustration must become an original generated bitmap with **no lettering, logos or brands inside the generated image**. Keep readable fictional titles, dynamic dates, Cast names, labels, instructions, prices, forms, rules and player writing as separate native typography. Do not rasterise player content into a shared generated asset. Zip is a **PURPLE BADGER, with a WHITE STRIPE, GOGGLES and TRAINERS** in every packaging, magazine, poster, sticker and disc variant. Preserve all existing runtime IDs/save keys. Old real/un-parodied source strings are audit evidence, not approved generation prompts.

Scope covers main-house print, media packaging, front/back/spine/inside faces, all named catalogue products, every authored physical reading page, separate old archive covers/pull-outs, VHS/cassettes, groceries, mail-order parcels, stationery/photos, mentioned-but-unmodelled toy/board-game packaging, digital printed cards and JackWorld print furnishings. Secondary printed decoration and authored Stamp Studio brushes are explicitly included. Ordinary game sprites, screen UI, operating-system icons, general encyclopedia/web illustrations, plain wood/plastic/fabric materials and player-created art/posts are outside the print/packaging replacement scope. Family printer outputs are dynamic documents; only their paper/decoration is an asset candidate.

**Paths:** source names below are relative to `app/public/js/` unless explicitly qualified. Every target is relative to `app/public/art/`. Existing folders are `box-v1`, `magazines-v1`, `posters-v3`, `print-v2`; the new registered generation folders are `box-v2`, `print-v3`, `magazines-v2`, `posters-v4`; other target folders are **proposals only**. Brace notation expands to individual files, e.g. `pc-v2/park/{hero,back-01}.webp`. Targets are art layers, not whole UI/printed text exports. Repeated target paths intentionally reuse an illustration. The catalogue page targets are optional page-background/collage layers; SKU hero art remains authoritative.

**IDs:** a listed runtime ID is preserved exactly. Names such as `cx.mag.01`, `letter.mom`, `coaster.packaging`, `st.diary.chapter1.0`, `carta.stamp.0` and `backlog.G09` are inventory identifiers for surfaces that have no existing standalone runtime ID; they must not be introduced as save IDs by accident. `pm.parcel.*` and `pm.fl.N` denote dynamic instances, not missing finite catalogue rows.

## Status and provenance

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| generated-compatible | Existing bitmap has explicit image_gen provenance and a no-type illustration brief. Reusable candidate; not a claim of full visual/character compliance. |
| generated-text-conflict | Explicitly generated, but lettering/logo/date is baked into the image. Regenerate textless art under the next version. |
| mixed-generated-source | Generated illustration is embedded in source-drawn/typeset print, or only some faces are generated. |
| source / legacy-source | Authored canvas, SVG, CSS or code-native pixel art; **not image-generated**. Legacy means an older/alternate presentation path. |
| missing-illustration | Physical/readable surface exists but lacks the requested illustration. |
| absent | Mention/proposal exists, but no matching printed model/artwork surface is wired. |
| uncertain-wiring | Texture/function is registered or prose implies a surface, but an active matching consumer was not established. |
| dynamic | Per-player text/state/documents; generate only reusable paper/background/illustration layers. |
| source-parody-review | Source art plus a display title requiring a fictional parody audit. |
| no-illustration-slot | Exact title indexed; current renderer is prose-only. Adding pictures requires runtime work. |

All 29 existing WebPs are accounted for: 3 box + 9 shared print + 5 posters + 12 magazines. Their manifests explicitly record built-in image_gen. The nine embedded `PrintArtData` payloads were decoded in memory and compared byte-for-byte with `print-v2/*.webp`: all nine match. Embedded data URLs are exports of generated source bitmaps, not another provenance class.

Visually inspected existing files: `box-v1/kart.webp`, `print-v2/critters.webp`, `magazines-v1/10.webp`, `posters-v3/band.webp`. The latter two visibly bake in their type. This was sample inspection, not approval of every asset; generated creature silhouettes still need the main agent's originality/consistency review.

## Inventory counts and use

The catalogue cross-check found **23 console games**, **17 Games Cupboard entries**, **47 Pixel Post shop entries after addCarts** (10 PC products + 12 monthly magazines + 4 hardware + 21 console products), **12 physical monthly magazines / 72 named interior pages**, **9 ReadingBooks documents / 36 named pages**, **30 Series One cards / 38 Byte Beasties**, **12 JackWorld print furnishing entries**, and **32 authored Stamp Studio stamps**. The two console games not mail-order listed are `sn.zero` and `sn.plumb`: their Super Ultra is marked away and they have no shop price. They remain in this art inventory.

Each row below carries item/title, current source/art family, target version folder and status. The section's “Used” and “Source” apply to every row. Faces state the print surfaces represented by that row; a shared hero can cover several faces without generating duplicate pictures.

## Baseline runtime findings (historical; current registry refresh above takes precedence)

The following numbered notes describe the initial source snapshot and are retained as audit context. The current registry and updated item rows supersede notes about six-only console wiring, lettered magazine/poster covers, missing medium-label integration and SVG embedding. Do not treat a historical finding as still open without checking the current row.

## Baseline runtime hand-off

1. **Console art is three independent paths.** Only six game IDs are in `caseArtFiles`: `n64.kart`, `n64.range`, `ps.run`, `ps.grav`, `gb.mon`, `gb.mon2`. They affect case fronts and the three repeated case-back thumbnails. `mediaLabel` still draws the generic checker crest for every non-Zip cartridge/disc. `PMArt.cartArt` independently draws catalogue gradients/circles for console products. Supplying a WebP to one path does not update the other two. Existing cases have title spines; there is no distinct generated manual art slot. The four Super Ultra rows (`sn.zero`, `sn.zeldo`, `sn.plumb`, `sn.fight`) are catalogue/game definitions: `mediaShelf` excludes every `away` console, so their individual case/medium print surfaces are not currently instantiated there. They still need art when physical Super Ultra media is wired.
2. **Zip has no generated packaging bitmap.** `md.zip` and `dc.dash` currently use source canvas art in `caseScene`, including the slogan “DIG THE SPEED!”. Their medium labels crop that same procedural portrait. The old archive October `ART.monster` also embeds a procedural Zip. New shared generated art must respect the purple badger/white stripe/goggles/trainers design in every crop; preserve game IDs.
3. **PC assets cover only three front heroes.** `Discs.front` maps `city→city`, `keepa→dungeon`, `podracer→hover` through `PrintArt.svg`. Box-back `D.SHOT[scene](0..2)` images remain procedural; price stickers, branding/type, spines, tray cards, manuals and labels are independently drawn. `PMArt.innerBig/flapInside` exist, but the current WebGL bigBox uses a plain inner tray/lid rather than invoking these old illustrated interior functions. Do not mark those interiors wired because the functions exist.
4. **Physical magazines and old archive differ.** `MagazineCat`'s 12 editorial issues are not the 12 `CXMag.META/DATA` archive issues. `PMArt.magCover` still embeds the old archive SVG, whereas `Magazines.bind/sync` replaces visible physical covers with `magazines-v1` art and supplies the modern physical reader. Jan–Jun legacy house aliases are preserved; other owned magazine props use `pmb.pm.mag.MM`. Cover-CD labels still draw from the older CXMag palette. Use explicit issue mappings, not a single interchangeable “magazine” family.
5. **All magazine interiors need a real art manifest.** Editorial/review pages crop the already-lettered front cover; guides all use one circuit-style drawing; postbags all use one envelope; adverts show the issue cover rather than their named fictional product; comics show a generic shared hero instead of their scripts' panels. The 72 exact titles/families above identify each missing deliverable. Article layout and all text should remain separate from generated pictures.
6. **Book pages are prose-only.** The six paperbacks, family directory, TV guide and Space Kat comic have no illustration hook in `pageTexture`'s non-magazine branch. Existing book covers reuse generic PrintArt families; their backs reuse their front. School notebooks have no ReadingBooks interior document. Space Kat needs actual sequential illustrated panels, not the current cat/planet cover and prose script alone.
7. **Paper catalogue and web shop need one SKU image adapter.** `PM.shopArt` returns an HTML `<img>` for magazine products; `PMArt.catPage` expects to embed SVG markup inside SVG. That nested HTML image is a source-level compatibility gap for printed magazine product pictures; no browser verification was performed here. Use a data/image layer compatible with both consumers. There are 47 products and 24 product pages with this source snapshot. `catalogPages` creates cover + intro + pages 3–26 + back cover + a padding order form (28 faces); `catalogueDoc` moves the padding order form inside for reading. Do not flatten the dynamic price/release text into art.
8. **Hardware retail cartons are largely missing.** `hardwareArt` supplies SVG web/catalogue product images. Actual deliveries are generic Pixel Post parcels that reveal bare hardware. The separate bedroom Daydream box is front-only print. No Game Lad/Super Ultra retail-box SKU, detailed hardware wrap/manual model, board-game retail box or toy-kit instruction reader was found.
9. **Parcel art is one shared template.** All purchased IDs use `pm.parcelf`; it is not 47 independently illustrated shipping labels. Dynamic recipient, item and signature stay separately rendered. The prize parcel has a different `rm.addr` canvas. Preserve tape, flaps, interior, removable shipping wrapper and identity repaint behaviour. Do not generate real addresses or fixed Cast names.
10. **Two VHS sets and several audio paths coexist.** Six playback VHS objects use PrintArt thumbnail families; six chore VHS objects have independent plain labels. Twelve Radio Mix cassette spines (3–14), two bedroom home recordings, the C90 radio tape, five old RoomBits albums, PM audio discs and JackCarta's four shoebox CDs each have separate consumers. There are no retail cassette J-card/sleeve models to silently assume.
11. **SVG embedding is an implementation requirement.** `PrintArt.svg` currently uses byte-identical embedded data URLs for standalone SVG-to-canvas decoding; simply changing standalone WebPs may leave embedded sources stale. `PMGL` rasterises SVG Blob URLs asynchronously and caches by texture key. New bitmap loads need decode/repaint/dirty handling and a working fallback. `printedCaseTexture` load callbacks repaint registered case textures, while `mediaLabel` is a separate plain `K.tex`.
12. **Parody review remains open.** Old archive metadata/reviews, `HouseArt.vhsSpines`, `ch.cdface` (NOW 43), old `rm-bits` poster artwork, JackWorld poster titles/miniature lettering and source-generated four-pane/spiral imagery need visual/text review. This document quotes current titles for identification; no real-brand title/likeness is approved for new artwork. Technical API names such as DirectX and font names are not automatically authored brand art.
13. **No model creation is implied by an absent row.** Toy/board-game/shoebox and proposed Zeldo rows make scope gaps visible without inventing owned items, IDs, saves or publication dates. “Backlog V14 shoebox” is a suggested replacement for a placeholder, not evidence of an existing branded shoebox. The G09 Ultra 64 title has no registered game ID yet.
14. **Functional printed labels and user art remain dynamic.** Calendar dates, timetable, diary checklists, wages, progress, forms, family printer documents, rules, odds, serial numbers, card stats and player floppy labels must not be baked into shared art. Native typography can compose with generated illustration/paper layers. Main agent owns all runtime and visual tests.

## Historical first-handoff verification and limits (26 October)

This section records the previous incomplete handoff, not the current continuation's test gate: 105 manifest-listed files existed, 100 registry keys resolved, and nine embedded/standalone old illustrations matched. All accepted originals were reviewed visually before export; rejected variants were not included. No old asset folders were overwritten.

- `node --check` on all 14 modified app JS files and new test scripts: exit 0. `git diff --check`: exit 0.
- `node tests/print-art-unit.cjs`: exit 0, 6/6 (deduplicated loading, aliases, parser deferral, aspect/clip, offline fallback, fallback-font masthead width and already-open page repaint). `node tests/art-gallery-unit.cjs`: exit 0, 7/7. Source audit: exit 0; 12 historical magazine manifests lack original PNG basenames, not new missing assets.
- First full gallery: exit 1 after **890 production bitmap/compositor exports, 77 contact sheets and 24 physical views**. Its sole report failure was the harness's CSP-incompatible waitForFunction at item-return cleanup, now replaced by evaluate polling without weakening the site's CSP. Captures include real shelves from oblique/front views, all five wall-poster close-ups, and PC-box hand/inspection/resizes. Full historical report/index are retained as `tests/shots/art-gallery/full-export-report.json` / `full-export-index.html` (ignored test output).
- Visual review caught a clipped monthly masthead, a cropped Zip head and clipped demo-release ribbons. The native layouts now fit the complete wording and whole console silhouettes. `node tests/art-native.mjs` on disposable data: **exit 0**; actually inspected `native-fixes-current.png` showing the current production magazine/poster and full-silhouette fit diagnostic. This diagnostic is not presented as a physical in-game model capture.
- `node tests/smoke.mjs`: **exit 1**, login/setup timeouts and 180s watchdog; not a green deploy gate. `node tests/smoke.mjs --only=server-log`: exit 0. Disposable server startup logs had no mod failed to load/unhandled/fatal messages. No live-site test, production account or production data was used.
- Focused world reruns did **not** pass. One was terminated (143); the smaller startup run was deliberately stopped (130) near the task limit. Its 61 fresh compositor exports / eight sheets exposed widespread retained procedural fallback art under heavy startup. Those patched-* sheets are **rejected regression evidence**, not approved generated-art captures. Root cause: the eight-second fetch timer could expire while room construction/parser work monopolised startup. The loader now waits for DOMContentLoaded and allows 60 seconds; a parser-order browser probe and native print regression passed (exit 0), with no missing generated keys. The full 3D startup after this last fix is **not verified** and is the first manager follow-up. The gallery now fails early if any registered key retained fallback art, including in world-only mode. Offline magazine fallback typography was also aligned/fitted.
- All owned disposable servers were stopped; their logs have no mod failed to load, unhandled or fatal entries. No production server was restarted. Capture filenames are overwritten across runs: historical report PNG hashes must be checked before treating a file as that run's evidence.

Reviewed compositor/contact-sheet samples span monthly covers, PC fronts and cover CDs, native editorial text, console cases/labels, Space Kat, packaging and posters. This is not visual approval of all 890 duplicated surfaces. All-prop physical coverage, fresh screenshots of every final layout, real-device touch, hardware FPS, full reader refresh/Esc/rapid-input paths and exhaustive model-face inspection remain unverified. No deployment, version bump, commit or push was performed. No production data or other worktree was changed.

## Continuation verification — 27 October (current handoff)

- All **122 bitmap files / 116 registry keys** pass the source audit (exit 0); nine legacy embedded copies still match their files. Seventeen new accepted outputs in `print-v4` were individually inspected before encoding and again in contact sheets. Manifests retain method, prompts, review notes, dimensions and byte counts. Original generation PNGs stay in the tool's generated-image cache; runtime uses only repository WebPs.
- `node tests/print-art-unit.cjs`: **exit 0, 9/9**. `node tests/art-gallery-unit.cjs`: **exit 0, 7/7**. `node tests/art-return-unit.cjs`: **exit 0, 3/3** (includes the former low-FPS failure). `node tests/board-art-unit.cjs`: **exit 0, 2/2**, exercising the actual carton geometry builder's UVs.
- Final `node --check`: **exit 0 on all 17 touched JS/test files**. `git diff --check`: **exit 0**. Conflict-marker scan: **none**. `node --expose-internals tests/parody-audit.cjs`: **exit 0, zero authored spans to rewrite**. The manager's merge index still needs staging; no Git write was attempted.
- Full `tests/art-gallery.mjs --all-world`: **exit 0**, **934 bitmap/compositor exports, 81 contact sheets, 288 physical views of all 62 available selected props**. Report: `tests/shots/art-gallery/2026-10-27T01-49-39-447Z/report.json`. All 116 registry statuses were generated, the material probe reported no retained-paint errors, and the browser/server module checks were clear. Twenty snapped PC cases passed visible-geometry overlap/upright checks. All 181 tested print textures passed Potato (maximum edge 256), Low (512), High restoration (1024 in this set), mipmap and anisotropy checks.
- That full run preceded the final typography and carton-UV refinements. A focused `--world-only --items=...` pass then covered all ten PC scene identities, Pixel Post, the formerly stuck party case and Space Dog: **exit 0**, **229 exports, 22 sheets, 77 physical views**. Report: `tests/shots/art-gallery/2026-10-27T02-12-10-340Z/report.json`. Reviewed the complete wrapped box-back copy and uncropped, paper-backed Pixel Post straplines; no browser/module errors.
- The board-game close-ups caught a mirror flip and mip-bleeding colour strips; the final geometry maps the lid unmirrored toward the room and samples one solid-ink texel for each side. `tests/art-board-review.mjs`: **exit 0**, four real-room angles plus reload, all generated mappings present, no browser/module errors. **Final board visual evidence supersedes the earlier full/focused board images:** `tests/shots/art-gallery/board-final-2026-10-27T02-21-17-284Z/{front,left,close,right,close-after-refresh}.png`.
- Requested **single** `SMOKE_SCALE=3 node tests/smoke.mjs`: **exit 0, 12/12** (signup, account/house, walk/refresh, PC controls, console game, phone typing, prop/refresh, Esc menu, canonical views, mobile-desktop check, browser errors and server log). Log: `tests/shots/art-gallery/smoke-scale3-2026-10-27.log`. All six fresh canonical PNGs in `tests/shots/smoke/` were viewed. Renderer: SwiftShader; draw calls were bedroom 770, desk 504, TV 385, landing 534, kitchen 357, front garden 1046. These are draw counts, **not hardware FPS**; the script's mobile check is not full real-device gameplay proof.
- Earlier continuation attempts are retained as rejected/incomplete evidence: the 01:21 run terminated with 143; the 01:28 all-world run failed after 211 views on an indefinitely bouncing returned case. The latter exposed the actual low-frame-rate contact-settling bug, now fixed and reproduced/covered by the landing unit test and successful subsequent physical passes. No failures were hidden by changing the return state in the harness.
- Captures include 1366×860, 1920×1080, 390×844 and 844×390. Narrow captures resize the same WebGL room; they do **not** establish real-phone startup, touch performance or the full device policy. This is sampled visual approval, not a claim that every duplicated compositor export or model face was manually inspected.
- New runs use timestamped output folders and a latest-run pointer; old gallery reports/captures are no longer overwritten. Browser tests ran sequentially on disposable localhost servers/free ports. Owned servers are stopped; their clear module logs are copied beside the successful gallery runs. No live site, production account/data, other worktree, release version, deployment, Git commit or push was changed by this continuation. The manager must stage the resolved files/finish the existing merge.

Still not finished: the secondary inventory noted at the top, independently commissioned illustrations for every interior page/tape, and missing toy/hardware retail-carton models. Lower board-game lids remain physically covered in the existing fixed stack; their artwork was checked in the native atlas, not by inventing new movable props. The original R31 leaning save was unavailable; current snapped geometry was verified, not that exact historic save. Hardware FPS, real-device touch/gamepads and exhaustive reader refresh/Esc/rapid-input paths were not verified by the art tests.
